\displaystyle\leq c_{1}(n,p)+n^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(x)}n_{x}(z)^{p-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{p}\varepsilon^{p\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ip}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{p}\right]

for some constant
c_{1}(n,p)>0
. Given
K,K_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, for each
i\in\{1,\cdots,K\}
, and real numbers
a_{ij}>0
for all
j\in\{1,\cdots,K_{i}\}
,
i\in\{1,\cdots,K\}
, we see by an application of Jensen’s inequality for the (concave) log function that

\prod_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K_{i}}a_{ij}\leq\left(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K_{i}}a_{ij}\right)^{K}.

Thus

\displaystyle\prod_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(x)}n_{x}(z)^{p-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{p}\varepsilon^{p\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ip}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(|\operatorname{supp}(x)|^{-1}\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(z)}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{p}\varepsilon^{p\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ip}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{p}\right)^{|\operatorname{supp}(x)|}.

By Jensen’s inequality and the fact that
1\leq|\operatorname{supp}(x)|\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\left(|\operatorname{supp}(x)|^{-1}\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(z)}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{p}\varepsilon^{p\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ip}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{p}\right)^{|\operatorname{supp}(x)|}

\displaystyle\leq|\operatorname{supp}(x)|^{-1}\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(z)}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{p}\varepsilon^{p\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ip}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{p}\right)^{|\operatorname{supp}(x)|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(z)}n_{x}(z)^{n-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{pn}\varepsilon^{pn\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ipn}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{pn}.

Taking expectations, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(z)}n_{x}(z)^{n-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{pn}\varepsilon^{pn\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ipn}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{pn}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(z)}n_{x}(z)^{n-1}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{x}(z)}s(n_{x}(z),i)^{pn}\varepsilon^{pn\kappa(n_{x}(z)-i)}i^{ipn}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{pn}\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}(n,p)\max_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}:n_{x}=n}\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\max_{i\in\{0,\cdots,n_{x}(z)\}}\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{pn}\right].

for some
c_{2}(n,p)>0
. Next, we apply (
4.17
) followed by Jensen’s inequality and (
4.16
) to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{i}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{pn}\right]\leq c_{3}(n,p)+c_{4}(n,p)\max_{j\in\{0,\cdots,i\}}\mathbb{E}\left[Q^{\varepsilon}_{jn}(\varepsilon^{-\kappa}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z))\right],

for some
c_{3}(n,p),c_{4}(n,p)>0
. The last expectation is finite uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
by Proposition
24
, which concludes the proof.
∎

4.3.
Uniform equicontinuity and tightness

The aim of this section is to prove (
4.2
), the second condition of Aldous’ tightness criterion, which is an immediate consequence of the following uniform equicontinuity estimate.

Proposition 29
.

Fix
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
, a family
\{\tau_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}
of
[0,T]
-valued stopping times, and
\{\theta_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}\subset[0,\infty)
with
\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\theta_{\varepsilon}=0
. Then, there exist
c=c(T,\varphi)>0
and
a>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(\varphi)-X^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(\varphi)|\right]\leq c\left(\varepsilon^{\kappa/2}+\max(\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/2},\varepsilon)+\theta_{\varepsilon}^{a}\right),

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

By Jensen’s inequality, we have

(4.18)

\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)|\right]\leq\varepsilon\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta}^{\varepsilon}(z)-X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z)|\right]\varphi(\varepsilon z)

If
x=\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\}}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
is the configuration with a single particle at
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, we have

X^{\varepsilon}_{t}(z)-X^{\varepsilon}_{s}(z)=Q^{\varepsilon}(x,X^{\varepsilon}_{t})-Q^{\varepsilon}(x,X^{\varepsilon}_{s}).

Thus by Lemma
25
,

\displaystyle Q^{\varepsilon}(x,X^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}})-Q^{\varepsilon}(x,X^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}[\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})]Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},X^{\varepsilon}_{0})

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{u}(x,x^{\prime})\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}-u}^{\varepsilon})du

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\tau_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}[\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}-u}(x,x^{\prime})]\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})du

\displaystyle+M^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(x)-M^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x).

Let
\overline{\theta}\coloneqq\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\theta_{\varepsilon}\in[0,\infty)
. It follows from Jensen’s inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[|X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z)-X_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z)|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}[\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})]Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},X^{\varepsilon}_{0})\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})|\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|du\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})||\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})|du\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\Big|M^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(x)-M^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x)|\right].

We control the four terms on the right-hand side in turn. Denote them by
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,
A_{3}
, and
A_{4}
, respectively. To bound
A_{1}
, we adapt a method used in Lemma 27 of
[
Etheridge and Labbé, 2015
]
and apply Assumption
A2
.b. By the semigroup property of
G^{\varepsilon}
, and the fact that
\mathbb{G}_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}(x,x^{\prime})=G_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})
for configurations
x=\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\}}
and
x^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z^{\prime}\}}
with a single particle at
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}[\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})]Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},X^{\varepsilon}_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime\prime})[\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(x,x^{\prime})]Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},X^{\varepsilon}_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})\left[\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime})X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})-\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})\right].

We then apply a change of variables to obtain

\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime})X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})=\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(0,z^{\prime})X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime}),

and similarly for the second inner summation. Hence, by Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(0,z^{\prime})|X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})-X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z)|\right].

Let
h^{\varepsilon}_{t}(z^{\prime})\coloneqq\min(1,\varepsilon t^{-1/2})e^{-z^{\prime}\min(1,\varepsilon t^{-1/2})}
where we think of
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
by abuse of notation, and where
t\in[0,T]
. By (
C.5
) in Lemma
49
, there exists
C=C(T)>0
such that

(4.19)

G^{\varepsilon}_{t}(0,z^{\prime})\leq Ch^{\varepsilon}_{t}(z^{\prime}),

for all
t\in[0,T]
,
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. It is easy to see that for any
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, we may find
t_{*}(z^{\prime})
, which maximises
h_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})
over
t\in[0,T]
. Moreover, there exists a deterministic natural number
R
such that for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}
with
z\geq R
, if
z^{\prime}=\pi^{\varepsilon}(z)
, then we have
t_{*}(z^{\prime})=T
. We also observe that
h^{\varepsilon}_{t_{*}(z^{\prime})}(z^{\prime})
for
z^{\prime}
among the first
R
elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
is maximised at
z^{\prime}=0
with maximum value one. Overall, this implies that with
C=C(T)>0
the constant from (
C.5
), we have the deterministic upper bound

(4.20)

G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(0,z^{\prime})\leq C\overline{h}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}),

where
\overline{h}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{\{0,1,\cdots,R-1\}\cap\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}(z^{\prime})+h^{\varepsilon}_{T}(z^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\{R,R+1,\cdots,K_{\varepsilon}-1\}\cap\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}
. Summing over
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
gives

\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(0,z^{\prime})\leq\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\overline{h}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})\leq CR+C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}\\
z^{\prime}\geq R\end{subarray}}\min(1,\varepsilon T^{-1/2})e^{-z^{\prime}\min(1,\varepsilon T^{-1/2})}.

The sum on the right-hand side converges as a Riemann sum to
\int_{R}^{\infty}T^{-1/2}e^{-zT^{-1/2}}dz<1
, so the right-hand side is bound uniformly in
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
and in
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
by a constant
C^{\prime}
which depends only on
T
. Importantly, the right-hand side of (
4.20
) is completely deterministic, hence

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\overline{h}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})-X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z)|\right].

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})-X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z)|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})-X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z)|^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

By Assumption
A2
.b, there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
depending only on
\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)|
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z_{1})-X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z_{2})|^{2}\right]\leq c_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa}+c_{2}|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z_{1})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z_{2})|^{2},

for all
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Recall from (
1.11
) in Definition
17
that
\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot)=\rho_{0}(K_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\cdot)
, where
\rho_{0}\in C^{3}(\mathbb{S}^{1},[0,2\mu])
, and hence it is Lipschitz-continuous with some constant
L_{0}>0
independent of
\varepsilon
. It follows that

\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})-X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}-z)|^{2}\right]\leq c_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa}+c_{2}L_{0}^{2}|z-z^{\prime\prime}|^{2},

for all
z,z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, where we note that the right-hand side does not depend on
z^{\prime}
. Then, using the facts that
\sqrt{a_{1}+a_{2}}\leq 2^{1/2}(\sqrt{a_{1}}+\sqrt{a_{2}})
for all
a_{1},a_{2}\geq 0
and
\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})=1
, we bound our first term as follows:

A_{1}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa/2}+C_{2}\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})|z^{\prime\prime}-z|,

where
C_{1}\coloneqq CC^{\prime}\sqrt{2c_{1}}
and
C_{2}\coloneqq CC^{\prime}L_{0}\sqrt{2c_{2}}
. We have
ye^{-y}\leq 1
for all
y\geq 0
. Then, by (
C.4
) in Lemma
49
, we compute

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa/2}+C_{2}\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})|z^{\prime\prime}-z|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa/2}+C_{2}\max(\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/2},\varepsilon)\sum_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\theta_{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime\prime})e^{|z^{\prime\prime}-z|/\max(\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/2},\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa/2}+C_{3}\max(\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/2},\varepsilon)

for some
C_{3}>0
. Next, we control
A_{2}
. By (
C.2
), Hölder’s inequality with
q\in(1,2)
and
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{q-1}{q}=1
, and Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})|\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|du

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{s}^{t}(t-u)^{-1/2}\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|du

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{s}^{t}(t-u)^{-q/2}du\right)^{1/q}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|^{\frac{q}{q-1}}du\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}.

Observe that
\int_{s}^{t}(t-u)^{-q/2}du=(t-s)^{1-q/2}/(1-q/2)
and
t-s\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}
. Using the concave Jensen inequality for
y\mapsto y^{\frac{q-1}{q}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{2-q}{2q}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]du\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{2-q}{2q}}\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]^{\frac{q-1}{q}}.

The latter expectation is bounded by a constant depending only on
T
and
q
. Indeed, a direct computation of the generator using (
4.6
) shows that if
x^{\prime}\coloneqq\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z^{\prime}\}}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, then

\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})=\mu_{\varepsilon}X_{u}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}X_{u}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})(X_{u}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime})-\varepsilon^{\kappa}),\quad u\geq 0.

We re-write this expression in terms of the
Q^{\varepsilon}
defined in (
1.5
) and apply Corollary
28
to Proposition
24
to obtain good control on its absolute moment. Let
x^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq 2\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z^{\prime}\}}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
be the configuration with two particles at
z^{\prime}
. Then,

\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{t}^{\varepsilon})=\mu_{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{2}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime\prime},X_{t}^{\varepsilon}),\quad t\geq 0.

Thus, by the triangle inequality, Jensen’s inequality, and Corollary
28
, there exists a constant
C=C(T,q)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X_{u}^{\varepsilon})|^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\Bigg(\mu_{\varepsilon}\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},X_{u}^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]

(4.21)

\displaystyle+\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime\prime}}=2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime\prime},X_{u}^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]\Bigg)<C,

uniformly in
u\in[0,T]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. We conclude that
A_{2}\leq C\theta_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{2-q}{2q}}
for some
C=C(T,q)>0
. Consider now
A_{3}
. By (
C.3
) from Lemma
49
, we obtain, for all
a\in(0,1/2)
, the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\left|G_{t-u}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})-G_{s-u}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})\right|

(4.22)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon f_{u}(s,t),

for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
x=\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\}}
and
x^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z^{\prime}\}}
,
a\in(0,1/2)
, and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and where

f_{u}(s,t)\coloneqq(s-u)^{-a-1/2}(t-s)^{a}+(s-u)^{-1/2}\Big((t-s)+(t-s)^{1/2}\Big).

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})||\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})|^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{1/2},

where

h^{\varepsilon}(u)\coloneqq\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})|^{2}.

By (
4.22
), we have

\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})|^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}f_{u}(s,t).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})||\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})|

\displaystyle\leq Cf_{u}(s,t)h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{1/2}.

We have shown that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}|\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-u}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s-u}(x,x^{\prime})||\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})|du

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{s}f_{u}(s,t)h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{1/2}du.

Next, we take
q\in(1,2)
and apply Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{q-1}{q}=1
:

(4.23)

\int_{0}^{s}f_{u}(s,t)h(u)^{1/2}du\leq\left(\int_{0}^{s}f_{u}(s,t)^{q}du\right)^{1/q}\left(\int_{0}^{s}h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}du\right)^{(q-1)/q}.

We bound these two factors in turn. By Jensen’s inequality and straightforward calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}f_{u}(s,t)^{q}du\leq 2^{q-1}(t-s)^{aq}\frac{s^{1-q(a+\frac{1}{2})}}{1-q(a+\frac{1}{2})}+2^{q-1}[t-s+(t-s)^{1/2}]\frac{s^{1-q/2}}{1-q/2}.

To avoid diverging terms as
s\searrow 0
, we require that
q<2/(2a+1)
- observe that this is ok since
2/(2a+1)\in(1,2)
for all
a\in(0,1/2)
. Hence, using that

(a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3})^{b}\leq 3^{b}\max(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})^{b}\leq 3^{b}(a_{1}^{b}+a_{2}^{b}+a_{3}^{b}),

for all
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b\geq 0
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{0}^{s}f_{u}(s,t)^{q}du\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\left\{C_{1}(t-s)^{a}+C_{2}(t-s)^{1/q}+C_{3}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{a}+C_{2}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/q}+C_{3}\theta_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2q}},

for some
C_{i}=C_{i}(T,a,q)>0
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Next, we control the second factor on the right-hand side of (
4.23
). We observe that
(q-1)/q<1
for all
q\in(1,2/(2a+1))
. We can therefore apply the concave Jensen inequality and extend the range of integration up to
T+\overline{\theta}
to obtain

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\int_{0}^{s}h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}du\right)^{(q-1)/q}\Bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T+\overline{\theta}}h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}du\Bigg]^{(q-1)/q}

We further note that
\frac{q}{2(q-1)}>1
for all
q\in(1,2/(2a+1))
. So by Jensen’s inequality and using Corollary
28
as we did in (
4.21
) above, we have

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T+\overline{\theta}}h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}du\Bigg]\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x^{\prime}}=1\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{T+\overline{\theta}}\mathbb{E}[|\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{u})|^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}]du\leq C,

for some constant
C=C(T,q)>0
, uniformly in
s\in[0,T]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Overall, we get

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T\\
t-s\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{0}^{s}f_{u}(s,t)^{q}du\right)^{1/q}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\int_{0}^{T+\overline{\theta}}h^{\varepsilon}(u)^{\frac{q}{2(q-1)}}du\right)^{(q-1)/q}\Bigg]

\displaystyle<C\left((t-s)^{a}+(t-s)^{1/q}+(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}^{a}+\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/q}+\theta_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2q}}\right),

for some
C=C(T,a,q)>0
. We now control
A_{4}
. One can check that the predictable quadratic variation of
M_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}(x)
is given by

\langle M_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}(x)\rangle_{t}-\langle M_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}(x)\rangle_{r}=\int_{r}^{t}\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{s}(x)ds,\quad 0\leq r\leq t,

where

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{s}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x^{\prime}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T-s}(x,x^{\prime})\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}(Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)^{2})(X^{\varepsilon}_{s})

\displaystyle-2Q^{\varepsilon}(x,X^{\varepsilon}_{s})\sum_{x^{\prime}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T-s}(x,x^{\prime})\mathscr{G}_{BC}^{\varepsilon}Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime},\cdot)(X^{\varepsilon}_{s}).

Observe that the right-hand side can be re-written as a linear combination of the terms
Q^{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime\prime},X_{s}^{\varepsilon})
, where
n_{x^{\prime\prime}}\in\{0,1,\cdots,2\}
. Thus, it follows from Jensen’s inequality, Corollary
28
and similar arguments to those leading to (
4.21
) above that there exists a constant
c=c(T)>0
with

\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{s}(x)^{2}]<c,

for all
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
n_{x}=1
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Using the Itô isometry, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and Tonelli’s Theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle A_{4}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}dM_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{s}(x)ds\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/4}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{s}(x)^{2}ds\right]^{1/4}\leq\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/4}\left(T\sup_{s\in[0,T+\overline{\theta}]}\mathbb{E}[\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{s}(x)^{2}]\right)^{1/4}.

This proves that
A_{4}\leq C\theta_{\varepsilon}^{1/4}
for some
C=C(T)>0
. Going back to (
4.18
), and using the four bounds just derived and the fact that
\varepsilon\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\varphi(\varepsilon z)|\to\int|\varphi|
, the proposition follows.
∎

5.
Proof of the central limit theorem

In this section, we prove the central limit theorem claimed in Theorem
9
: the non-equilibrium fluctuations field

Y_{t}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)=\varepsilon^{1+\gamma-\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\left(X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)\right]\right)\varphi(\varepsilon z),\quad\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1}),\quad t\geq 0,

converges in distribution in the Skorohod topology on
\mathscr{D}([0,T],C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{\prime})
to the unique (in law) solution to the the stochastic partial differential equation

(5.1)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}Y=\Big(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}^{2}+\mu-\rho\Big)Y+\sqrt{\rho}\partial_{z}\dot{W}^{1}+\sqrt{(\sigma^{2}+\mu^{2})\rho+\rho^{2}/2}\dot{W}^{2}&\textnormal{on }[0,T]\times\mathbb{S}^{1},\\
Y_{t}\overset{d}{\to}Y_{0}\textnormal{ as }t\searrow 0,\end{cases}

where
\rho
, the hydrodynamic limit, solves (
1.1
) the Cauchy problem for the FKPP equation,
\dot{}\mathrm{W}^{i}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, are independent Gaussian space-time white noises, and
Y_{0}
is a
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{\prime}
-valued centred Gaussian variable with variance

\mathbb{E}[Y_{0}(\varphi)^{2}]=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}}\varphi(z)^{2}\rho_{0}(z)dz,\quad\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1}).

We follow the strategy outlined in Section
2
.

5.1.
Boltzmann-Gibbs principle and a corollary

The goal of this section is to prove the Boltzmann-Gibbs principle of Proposition
20
and to derive one of its implications which will be useful to show tightness of the fluctuations. In preparation for the application of that corollary in the next section, we work with slightly more general test functions than
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
. We introduce for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
the generalised test functions

\varphi^{\varepsilon}:\{(r,t)\in[0,T]^{2}:0\leq r\leq t\}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}\to\mathbb{R},\quad((r,t),z)\mapsto\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(z),

where for each in
t
and
z
, the function is assumed to be continuous in
r
. We also assume that
\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
for all
r
and
t
, and that

(5.2)

C_{\varphi}=C_{\varphi}(T)\coloneqq\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{0\leq r\leq t\leq T}\|\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty}<\infty,

where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes the supremum norm on
\mathbb{S}^{1}
. We briefly recall the claim of Proposition
20
. Let
T>0
,
\gamma\in[-1/2,-5/16)
, and
\kappa\in[0,1+2\gamma]
. Consider the non-linear fluctuations field

(5.3)

F^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon^{1+\gamma-\kappa}}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\left(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\varepsilon^{\kappa})-\mathbb{E}[X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\varepsilon^{\kappa})]\right)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z),

for all
0\leq r\leq t
. We show that

(5.4)

\limsup_{S\to\infty}\limsup_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\Bigg(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}S}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\Bigg)^{2}\right]=0.

To prove this result, we adapt the proof of Proposition 3.1 in
[
Boldrighini et al., 1992
]
. The main new challenge is the fact that the local carrying capacity of our population is of order
O(\varepsilon^{-\kappa})
, instead of simply
O(1)
in the work of Boldrighini et al. where
\kappa=0
. We are however able to control this larger population using Theorem
21
, our version of the quantitative convergence result for the
v
-functions. Indeed, in the weak competition regime with competition exponent
\kappa
, we find that the rate at which the
v
-functions of degree three or higher vanish improves precisely by a factor of
\varepsilon^{\kappa}
. For
v
-functions of degree one or two, we use that the second moment of the
V
-functions is still of order
\varepsilon^{\kappa}
.

Proof of Proposition
20
.

Throughout this proof, given a natural number
j
and a site
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, we will denote
x^{j,z}=j\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\}}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
the configuration with
j
particles at
z
. Recalling that

V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X^{\varepsilon}_{r};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})=(X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(z)-\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{2}-\varepsilon^{\kappa}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z),

we easily show by adding and subtracting
\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}
that

(5.5)

F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})=\frac{\varepsilon^{1+\gamma-\kappa}}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\big\{V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})-\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})]\big\}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z).

By Theorem
21
, we have
\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})]=O(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})
uniformly in
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
and
r\in[0,t]
. Thus this term gives a negligible contribution to the left-hand side of (
5.4
). It follows that the Boltzmann-Gibbs principle (
5.4
) is equivalent to

(5.6)

\limsup_{S\to\infty}\limsup_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}A_{\varepsilon}(s,t,S)=0,

where

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}(s,t,S)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr^{\prime}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r^{\prime},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},X_{r^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon})].

We change variables in the inner integral using
\widetilde{r}=r^{\prime}-r
and use even symmetry of the region of integration about the diagonal
r=r^{\prime}
to pull out a factor of
2
:

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}(s,t,S)

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},X_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon})].

By the tower law of expectation and the Markov property, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},X_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},X_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon})|X_{r}^{\varepsilon}]].

Proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
22
, we derive the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},X_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon})|X_{r}^{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho^{\varepsilon}_{r})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\widetilde{r}}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}-s}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(x_{1})}\sum_{h=-n_{x_{1}}(z)}^{1}c_{h}^{\varepsilon}(n_{x_{1}}(z),\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon}(z))\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z,h)},X_{r+s}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon})|X_{r}^{\varepsilon}].

Denote the integral on the right-hand side by
I_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{r})
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})I_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{r})]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\widetilde{r}}ds\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}-s}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(x_{1})}\sum_{h=-n_{x_{1}}(z)}^{1}c_{h}^{\varepsilon}(n_{x_{1}}(z),\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon}(z))\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z,h)},X_{r+s}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon})|X_{r}^{\varepsilon}]\right]\Bigg].

Moreover, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z,h)},X_{r+s}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon})|X_{r}^{\varepsilon}]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z,h)},X_{r+s}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z,h)},X_{r+s}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon})\right]^{1/2}

By Definition
12
, Corollary
28
, and part two of Lemma
15
, the right-hand side is dominated by a constant which depends only on
T
, uniformly in
r,s\in[0,T]
,
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
,
h\geq 1
, and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Similarly, by point two of Lemma
15
, the coefficients
c_{h}^{\varepsilon}(n_{x_{1}}(z),\rho_{r+s}^{\varepsilon}(z))
are uniformly bounded in absolute value by some constant which depends only on
T
. Overall, we get

\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})I_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{r})]=O(\widetilde{r})=O(\varepsilon^{2}S),

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},X_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r+\widetilde{r}}^{\varepsilon})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho^{\varepsilon}_{r})]+O(\varepsilon^{2}S).

Using (
5.2
), we see that the big-
O
term contributes to
A_{\varepsilon}(s,t,S)
the quantity

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})O(\varepsilon^{2}S)

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{1-\kappa+1+2\gamma-\kappa}S)C_{\varphi}^{2}

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{1-\kappa+1+2\gamma-\kappa}S),

which vanishes as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and then
S\to\infty
, since
\kappa\in[0,1+2\gamma]
with
\kappa<3/8
because
{\gamma\in[-1/2,-5/16)}
. Thus

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}(s,t,S)

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho^{\varepsilon}_{r})]+o(1),

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, then
S\to\infty
. To control the remaining integral, we distinguish three different cases in terms of
x_{1}
:
n_{x_{1}}(z)=2
,
n_{x_{1}}=0
, and
n_{x_{1}}(z)=1
. Suppose first that
x_{1}=x^{2,z}=2\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\}}
. Then, we need to control

\displaystyle A_{0,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x^{2,z})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})^{2}].

By the Green function estimate (
C.2
), we have the bound

\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x^{2,z})\leq C_{1}G^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(z^{\prime},z)\varepsilon/\sqrt{\widetilde{r}},

for some
C_{1}=C_{1}(T)
. Next, we show that for some
C_{2}=C_{2}(T)>0
,

(5.7)

\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})^{2}]\leq C_{2}\varepsilon^{\kappa},

uniformly in
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
and
r\in[0,T]
. To see this, we note that by a direct computation

\displaystyle V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle=V^{\varepsilon}(x^{4,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\Bigg[6X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{3}+6X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}-12X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-2X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{2}+13\varepsilon^{\kappa}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}\Bigg].

We take the expectation of both sides and apply Theorem
21
and part two of Lemma
15
, which yields

\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})^{2}]=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbb{E}[f(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z),\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z))],

where

f(x,\rho)=6x^{3}+6x\rho^{2}-12x^{2}\rho-2x(x-\rho)^{2}+13\varepsilon^{\kappa}x^{2}.

By Corollary
28
, we have

\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\mathbb{E}\left[f(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z),\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z))\right]|<\infty,

which proves (
5.7
). Using the Green function estimate above, the bound (
5.7
), the fact that
{\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(z^{\prime},z)=1}
for all
z^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{r}
, and (
5.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x^{2,z})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})^{2}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}C_{2}C_{\varphi}^{2}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-\kappa}/\sqrt{\widetilde{r}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle|A_{0,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon/\sqrt{\widetilde{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\frac{4}{3}(\varepsilon^{2}S)^{3/2}\varepsilon=CS^{-1/2},

where
C\coloneqq C_{1}C_{2}C_{\varphi}^{2}
. Thus
A_{0,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and then
S\to\infty
. Next, we assume that
x_{1}(z)=0
, meaning that we have to control

\displaystyle A_{1,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\\
x_{1}(z)=0\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})].

By part one of Lemma
46
and Theorem
21
, we have

\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})]=\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z}+x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})]=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}),

as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
, uniformly in
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
,
x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
n_{x_{1}}=2
, and
r\in[0,T]
. Using (
5.2
) and the fact that
\sum_{x_{1}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})<1
, this implies that

\displaystyle|A_{1,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)|

\displaystyle=o(\varepsilon^{1+2\gamma-\kappa})C_{\varphi}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r},

\displaystyle=o(\varepsilon^{1+2\gamma-\kappa}),

which tends to zero as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
since
\kappa\in[0,1+2\gamma]
. The last case to check is when
n_{x_{1}}(z)=1
, for which we control

\displaystyle A_{2,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}S}\right)^{2}\int_{s}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}S}dr\int_{0}^{s-r+\varepsilon^{2}S}d\widetilde{r}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\\
n_{x_{1}}(z)=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})].

We write
x_{1}=x^{1,z}+x_{1}^{\prime}
for some configuration
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with a single particle not located at
z
. By part three of Lemma
46
, we obtain

V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x^{1,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})=\sum_{j=0}^{3}c_{j}^{\varepsilon}V^{\varepsilon}(x^{j,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}).

The first point in Lemma
46
then implies

\displaystyle V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{\prime},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{j=0}^{3}c_{j}^{\varepsilon}V^{\varepsilon}(x^{j,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{3}c_{j}^{\varepsilon}V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{\prime}\cup x^{j,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon}).

By Theorem
21
, we have
\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{\prime}\cup x^{j,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})]=O(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})
uniformly in
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
,
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
r\in[0,T]
since the configuration
x_{1}^{\prime}\cup x^{j,z}
contains at least one particle. We note using the decomposition
x_{1}=x^{1,z}+x_{1}^{\prime}
and the Green function estimate (
C.2
) that

\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})=G^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(z^{\prime},z)\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{1,z^{\prime}},x_{1}^{\prime})\leq C\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{1,z^{\prime}},x_{1}^{\prime})\varepsilon/\sqrt{\widetilde{r}},

for some
C=C(T)>0
. Hence, using that
\sum_{x_{1}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{1,z^{\prime}},x_{1}^{\prime})<1
and that
\kappa\in[0,1+2\gamma]
, we bound the integrand of
A_{2,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)
as follows:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r+\widetilde{r},t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=2\\
n_{x_{1}}(z)=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{\widetilde{r}}(x^{2,z^{\prime}},x_{1})\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})V^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon})]\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq O(\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-\kappa})C_{\varphi}^{2}/\sqrt{\widetilde{r}}

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon)/\sqrt{\widetilde{r}},

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
\widetilde{r}\in[0,t-r+\varepsilon^{2}S]
. This estimate a similar calculation as for
A_{0,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)
above show that
|A_{2,\varepsilon}(s,t,S)|=O(S^{-1/2})
uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
which vanishes when
S\to\infty
. Thus (
5.6
) holds, which concludes the proof of the Proposition. ∎

In the proof of the central limit theorem, we will control time integrals of
F^{\varepsilon}_{r}
using the following corollary to the Boltzmann-Gibbs principle.

Corollary 30
.

Fix
0\leq\theta\leq\overline{\theta}
and let
t\in[0,T+\overline{\theta}]
and
s\in[0,T]
with
0\leq t-s\leq\theta
. Take a generalised test function
\varphi^{\varepsilon}
as in Proposition
20
. Then, there exist constants
c_{i}=c_{i}(T,C_{\varphi})>0
,
i=1,2
, such that

(5.8)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau\in[s,t]}\left(\int_{s}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]\leq c_{1}\theta^{2}+c_{2}\varepsilon^{4+2\gamma-2\kappa},

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Additionally, for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
, there exist
c_{i}=c_{i}(T,\varphi)>0
,
i=1,2
such that

(5.9)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right]\leq c_{1}\theta^{2}+c_{2}\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa},

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

We use Proposition
20
and the assumption that
0\leq t-s\leq\theta
to control the expectation on the left-hand side of (
5.8
). We partition the time integral over intervals of length
\varepsilon^{2}
, plus an additional shorter one:

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr

\displaystyle+\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr.

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor\frac{1}{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr

\displaystyle+\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\rho_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr.

By the inequality
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and then Jensen’s inequality on the first term, we obtain

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{s}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{4}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(t-s)\rfloor\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}

(5.11)

\displaystyle+2\left(\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}.

Taking the supremum over
\tau\in[s,t]
and using that
0\leq t-s\leq\theta
, we find

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau\in[s,t]}\left(\int_{s}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\theta\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}\theta\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]

(5.13)

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau\in[s,t]}\left(\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right].

We first control the expectation of the first term on the right-hand side. We have

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{2}\theta\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}\theta\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\theta^{2}\max_{k\in\{1,\cdots,\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}\theta\rfloor\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]

By Proposition
20
, the right-hand side is bounded by a constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(T)>0
uniformly over
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
k\in\{1,\cdots,\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}\theta\rfloor\}
, and
s\in[0,T+\theta]
. So

2\varepsilon^{2}\theta\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}\theta\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{s+\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]\leq c_{1}\theta^{2}.

We now bound the expectation of the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.13
). Recall from (
5.5
) that

F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})=\frac{\varepsilon^{1+\gamma-\kappa}}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\big\{V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{r}^{\>\varepsilon})-\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{r}^{\varepsilon};\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon})]\big\}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}(\varepsilon z).

We introduce the notation
\tau^{\prime}\coloneqq s+\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor
and make the change of variables
r^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{-2}(r-\tau^{\prime})
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2+\gamma-\kappa}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\big\{V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{\tau^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{2}r^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon};\widetilde{\rho}_{\tau^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{2}r^{\prime}}^{\>\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x^{2,z},X_{\tau^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{2}r^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon};\widetilde{\rho}_{\tau^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{2}r^{\prime}}^{\>\varepsilon})]\big\}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{\tau^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{2}r^{\prime},u}(\varepsilon z)dr^{\prime}.

We remark that
\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\leq 1
. Therefore, by Jensen’s inequality twice, assumption (
5.2
) on the test function, the fact that the second moment of
V^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded by Corollary
28
, and part two of Lemma
15
, we obtain for some
{c_{2}=c_{2}(T,C_{\varphi})>0}
the bound

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau\in[s,t]}\left(\int_{s+\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(\tau-s)\rfloor}^{\tau}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]dr\right)^{2}\right]\leq c_{2}\varepsilon^{4+2\gamma-2\kappa}.

This shows (
5.8
). For (
5.9
), we use the decomposition

(5.14)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}\int_{\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{\varepsilon^{2}\left(\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\right)}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr

\displaystyle+\int_{\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}^{t}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr.

Then if
\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\leq\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}[F^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi)-Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\widetilde{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq 9\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{k=\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}\int_{\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+9\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{s}^{\varepsilon^{2}\left(\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\right)}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+9\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{\varepsilon^{2}\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}^{t}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right].

We control each term in turn. For the first term, by Jensen’s inequality and the fact that
\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\leq\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{k=\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}\int_{\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left\{\left(\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor-\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}s\rfloor\right)\sum_{k=\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}\left(\int_{\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}\theta\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}(T+\overline{\theta})\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right].

By Proposition
20
, the expectation is bounded by a constant
C>0
depending on
T+\overline{\theta}
and
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}
, uniformly in
k\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}T\rfloor\}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Thus

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left\{\left(\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor-\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}s\rfloor\right)\sum_{k=\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor}\left(\int_{\varepsilon^{2}(k-1)}^{\varepsilon^{2}k}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right\}\right]\leq\theta(T+\overline{\theta})C.

The last two terms are similar so we only show the details for the first one. By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{s}^{\varepsilon^{2}\left(\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\right)}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]dr\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq t\leq T+\overline{\theta}\\
t-s\leq\theta\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{s}^{\varepsilon^{2}\left(\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\right)}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}\varphi)]^{2}dr\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\varepsilon^{4}\sup_{r\in[0,T+\overline{\theta}+1]}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi)]^{2}\right].

By (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi)]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{6+2\gamma-2\kappa}}{4}\left(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}\varphi(\varepsilon z)-\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(1+\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z))\varphi(\varepsilon z)\right)^{2}.

By part two of Lemma
15
, there is a constant
C=C(T+\overline{\theta}+1)>0
such that
|\widetilde{\rho}_{r}^{\>\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)|\leq C
uniformly in
r\in[0,T+\overline{\theta}+1]
. Therefore, using the elementary inequalities
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\right)^{2}
for
a_{i}\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi)]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{6+2\gamma-2\kappa}}{2}\left[\bigg(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)\bigg)^{2}(1+C)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}}{2}(2+C)\bigg(\varepsilon\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)\bigg)^{4}.

The process
\left(\varepsilon\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)\right)_{t\geq 0}
is stochastically dominated by
\overline{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}\overline{N}_{t}^{\varepsilon}
, where
(\overline{N}_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
is a pure birth process with transitions
\overline{N}\mapsto\overline{N}+\ell
at rate
\overline{N}p_{\ell}^{\varepsilon}
and started from
{\overline{N}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}N_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)}
where
\mathrm{N}^{\varepsilon}
is the re-scaled SLBP (Definition
3
). One easily computes the semimartingale decomposition of
\overline{X}^{\varepsilon}
:

\overline{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\overline{X}^{\varepsilon}_{0}+\mu_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\overline{X}^{\varepsilon}_{s}ds+M_{t}^{\varepsilon},

for some martingale
(M^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
. Taking the supremum over
t\in[0,T]
, the fourth power, and then expectations, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\overline{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{4}\right]\leq 3^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\overline{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{4}\right]+3^{4}\mu_{\varepsilon}^{q}T^{3}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{r\in[0,s]}\left(\overline{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{4}\right]ds+3^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(M_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{4}\right].

By Assumption
A2
.a, the first of the three terms on the right-hand side is bounded by a constant uniformly in
\varepsilon
. By
[
Lenglart et al., 2004
, page 37, second inequality of second display]
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(M_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{4}\right]\leq c\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\langle M_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{T}^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|M_{t}^{\varepsilon}-M_{t-}^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\right]\right),

for some constant
c>0
, where
\langle M^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot}\rangle_{T}
the predictable quadratic variation of
M^{\varepsilon}
is given by

\langle M^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{T}=\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})\int_{0}^{T}\overline{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}ds.

By Jensen’s inequality and
x^{2}\leq 1+x^{4}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\langle M_{\cdot}^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{T}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2+2\kappa}(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{2}T\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{r\in[0,s]}(\overline{X}_{r})^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2+2\kappa}(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})^{2}\left(T^{2}+T\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{r\in[0,s]}(\overline{X}_{s})^{4}\right]ds\right).

Moreover, we observe that
|M_{t}^{\varepsilon}-M_{t-}^{\varepsilon}|=\overline{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}-\overline{X}_{t-}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}L(\varepsilon)\leq\varepsilon
almost surely. It follows from Grönwall’s inequality that

C^{\prime}\coloneqq\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\overline{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\right)^{4}\right]<\infty.

Hence,

\mathbb{E}\left[\varepsilon^{4}\sup_{r\in[0,T+\overline{\theta}+1]}[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi)]^{2}\right]\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}}{2}(2+C)C^{\prime}.

This proves (
5.9
) assuming
\lceil\varepsilon^{-2}s\rceil+1\leq\lfloor\varepsilon^{-2}t\rfloor
. Otherwise, we have
0\leq t-s\leq 2\varepsilon^{\kappa}
, and the method just used applies directly to the left-hand side of (
5.14
), implying (
5.9
). This concludes the proof of the corollary.
∎

5.2.
Moments and tightness

The aim of this section is to show tightness of the SLBP fluctuations process introduced in Definition
7
.

Proposition 31
.

The family
\{\mathrm{Y}^{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}
is tight in
\mathscr{D}([0,T],C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})^{\prime})
.

By Mitoma’s Theorem (Theorem 4.1 in
[
Mitoma, 1983
]
), Proposition
31
follows from tightness of
\{Y^{\varepsilon}(\varphi):\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}
for each
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
. By Aldous’ Criterion (Theorem 16.10 and equation (16.32) in
[
Billingsley, 1999
]
), it suffices to show the following two conditions are satisfied for each
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
.

1.

For all
t\in[0,T]
,
\{Y^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\varphi):\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}
is tight in
\mathbb{R}
.

2.

Fix any family
{\{\tau_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}}
of
[0,T]
-valued stopping times and a family
{\{\theta_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}\subset[0,\infty)}
with
\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\theta_{\varepsilon}=0
. Then, for any
\eta>0
, we have

(5.15)

\limsup_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\mathbb{P}\left(|Y^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}+\theta_{\varepsilon}}(\varphi)-Y^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{\varepsilon}}(\varphi)|>\eta\right)=0.

The most important tool in the proof is Corollary
30
of the previous section which allows us to control the non-linear terms in the generator of the non-equilibrium fluctuations
\mathrm{Y}^{\varepsilon}
. To apply this corollary in our proof of tightness of
\{\mathrm{Y}^{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in(0,1)\}
, we need to introduce a term of the form
Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi)
, for some
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
, which is not originally present in the generator of
\mathrm{Y}^{\varepsilon}
. To this end, given
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
possibly dependent on
\varepsilon
, we study a martingale problem corresponding to
(Y^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}))_{s\in[0,t]}
for a specific test function of the form

\varphi^{\varepsilon}:\{(s,t)\in[0,\infty)^{2}:0\leq s\leq t\}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}\to\mathbb{R},\quad((s,t),z)\mapsto\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(z),

defined as follows. Recall that
\pi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{S}^{1}
denotes the canonical projection map, and define
\mathbb{S}^{1,\varepsilon}\coloneqq\pi([0,1]\cap K_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\mathbb{Z})
. We define
\varphi^{\varepsilon}
as the unique solution to the backward terminal value problem

(5.16)

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(z)+\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(z)(\mu_{\varepsilon}-\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{s}(z))\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}(z)=0,&s\in[0,t),\quad z\in\mathbb{S}^{1,\varepsilon},\\
\varphi_{t,t}^{\varepsilon}(z)=\varphi(z),&z\in\mathbb{S}^{1,\varepsilon},\end{cases}

where the discrete Laplacian
\Delta^{\varepsilon}
is defined on all functions
\psi:\mathbb{S}^{1}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\Delta^{\varepsilon}\psi(z)\coloneqq\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\psi(z+K_{\varepsilon}^{-1})+\psi(z-K_{\varepsilon}^{-1})-2\psi(z)\right),\quad z\in\mathbb{S}^{1}.

We then extend the definition of
\varphi^{\varepsilon}
to all of
\mathbb{S}^{1}
by choosing an arbitrary
C^{3}
interpolation. In Appendix
C.3
, we show existence and uniqueness of a solution which is continuous in
s
and
t
, and we prove a priori uniform bounds on the solution. We also show convergence to a limiting problem. In the following lemma, we introduce a useful martingale.

Lemma 32
.

Recall the definition of the non-linear fluctuations field
F^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.3
). For any
t\geq 0
, the process
(M^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon}))_{s\in[0,t)}
defined by

(5.17)

M^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})\coloneqq Y^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\varepsilon})-Y^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t})-\int_{0}^{s}\left(Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})\right)dr,

for
s\in[0,t)
, is a martingale with predictable quadratic variation

(5.18)

\langle M^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})\rangle_{s}=\int_{0}^{s}\Gamma_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})dr,

where

(5.19)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)^{2}\left[X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\varepsilon^{\kappa})+X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z)\bigg[\left(\nabla^{\varepsilon,-}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)\right)^{2}+\left(\nabla^{\varepsilon,+}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)\right)^{2}\bigg],

where
\nabla^{\varepsilon,\pm}
are the first order discrete differentials defined in Lemma
44
.

Proof.

For any
t\in[0,T]
, the process
\mathrm{M}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})
defined by

M^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})\coloneqq Y^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\varepsilon})-Y^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t})-\int_{0}^{s}({\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})+\partial_{s}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot,t})(r))dr,\quad s\in[0,t),

where
{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}
is the generator of
\mathrm{Y}^{\varepsilon}
computed in (
A.5
), is a martingale. Let
\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}+\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}
denote the generator of the rescaled SLBP from (
4.4
)-(
4.7
). By (
A.5
) and (
A.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle={\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-\varepsilon^{\gamma-\kappa}\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]

\displaystyle+{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-\varepsilon^{\gamma-\kappa}\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})].

Using that
\varphi^{\varepsilon}
solves (
5.16
), we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{s}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot,t})(r)

\displaystyle=Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}((\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}-\mu_{\varepsilon})\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-\frac{1}{2}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\Delta^{\varepsilon}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}).

It follows from Lemma
44
with the linear test function
f(y)=y
,
y\in\mathbb{R}
, that

(5.20)

\partial_{s}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot,t})(r)=Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}((\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}-\mu_{\varepsilon})\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})+\varepsilon^{\gamma-\kappa}\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})].

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})+\partial_{s}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot,t})(r)

\displaystyle={\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-\varepsilon^{\gamma-\kappa}\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{BC}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r,t})]+Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}((\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}-\mu_{\varepsilon})\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})

\displaystyle=Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t}),

where in the second step, we have applied Lemma
45
with the linear test function. This proves that (
5.17
) is a martingale. Moreover, one can check that its predictable quadratic variation is

\langle M^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})\rangle_{s}=\int_{0}^{s}\Gamma_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})dr,

where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})^{2})+\partial_{s}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})(r))^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-2Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})\Big[{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})(r))\Big],

for all
r\in[0,t]
. We simplify the first two terms on the right-hand side of this expression using (
A.5
), (
A.6
), (
5.20
) and Lemma
45
:

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})^{2})+\partial_{s}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon})(r))^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}(Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})^{2})-2\varepsilon^{\gamma-\kappa}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]-2Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\Delta^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+2Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})\Big[Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)^{2}\left[X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\varepsilon^{\kappa})+2X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(z)(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right].

By Lemma
44
and the assumption that
1+2\gamma-\kappa=0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}(Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})^{2})-2\varepsilon^{\gamma-\kappa}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})\mathbb{E}[\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon}_{RW}X_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})]-2Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\Delta^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}X_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z)\bigg[\left(\nabla^{\varepsilon,-}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)\right)^{2}+\left(\nabla^{\varepsilon,+}\varphi_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)\right)^{2}\bigg].

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The next lemma gives us control on the second moment of the fluctuations.

Lemma 33
.

There exists
c>0
depending on
T
,
\varphi
, and the uniform bound of Lemma
51
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\varphi)^{2}\right]<c,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\Delta^{\varepsilon}\varphi)^{2}\right]<c,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi)^{2}\right]<c,

for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

We first control
\mathbb{E}[Y^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\varphi)^{2}]
. By Lemma
32
, we have the semimartingale decomposition

Y^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\varepsilon})=Y^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t})+\int_{0}^{s}\left(Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})\right)dr+M^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{\cdot,t}^{\varepsilon}),

for any
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
. We let
s=t
, take the square on both sides, take expectations, and use the inequality
(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i})^{2}\leq n\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\varphi^{\>\varepsilon}_{0,t})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left[F_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})-Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\>\varepsilon}_{r}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{r,t})\right]dr\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[M^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{\cdot,t})^{2}\right]\Bigg).

Denote these three terms
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
, and
A_{3}
, respectively. We bound them in turn. Recall from Definition
17
that
\mathbb{E}[X_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)]=\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)
for all
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
. Thus by Definition
7
, (
1.7
) the formula for order two
V
-functions with particles on distinct sites, and Definition
13
, we have

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}[(X^{\varepsilon}_{0}(z)-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z))(X^{\varepsilon}_{0}(z^{\prime})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{\prime}))]\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t}(\varepsilon z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}[(X^{\varepsilon}_{0}(z)-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{2}]\varphi_{0,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z)^{2}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2+2\gamma-2\kappa}\sum_{z\not=z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}v_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x^{1,z}+x^{1,z^{\prime}}|\nu^{\varepsilon})\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{0,t}(\varepsilon z)\varphi_{0,t}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon z^{\prime}).

By Definition
17
, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that

(5.21)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)|\leq c_{0}.

Thus, by the explicit computation of a
V
-function of order two in (
1.8
), Assumption
A2
.a, and
\kappa\in[0,3/8)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(X^{\varepsilon}_{0}(z)-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{2}]\leq\varepsilon^{\kappa}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)+c\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}\leq c_{1}\varepsilon^{\kappa}.
