z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
. By a union bound and Chebyshev’s inequality, we obtain for any
\zeta>0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)|>\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}-\zeta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{P}(|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)|>\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}-\zeta})

\displaystyle\leq C(m_{0}+1)^{2}\varepsilon^{2N\zeta}.

We take
N>0
large enough that
2N\zeta>q
. This implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)|>\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}-\zeta}\right)<C(m_{0}+1)^{2}\varepsilon^{q}.

Let
\delta_{*}^{\prime}\in(0,\delta_{*}-\zeta)
. It follows from the bounds above that, on the event
\Omega_{2,0}=\{\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z)|<\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}-\zeta}\}
intersected with
\Omega_{1}
, we have

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\Delta^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},z)|<\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}.

Since
t\mapsto\Delta_{0}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)
is continuous and bounded in absolute value by one up to time

\tau_{0}^{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\sup\{t\geq\underline{t}^{\varepsilon}:\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\Delta_{0}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)|<1\},

then it is bounded from above, pointwise, by the solution to

y(t)=\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}+M_{0}\int_{s_{0}}^{t}y(s)ds,\quad t\in[\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},\tau_{0}^{\varepsilon}),

where
M_{0}=M_{0}(T,p)=C(m_{0}+1)^{2}
, for some constant
C=C(T,p)>0
. So
y(t)=\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M_{0}(t-\underline{t}^{\varepsilon})}
, and for
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we obtain

y(\tau^{\varepsilon}_{0})=1\iff\tau_{0}^{\varepsilon}\geq\underline{t}^{\varepsilon}+\delta_{*}^{\prime}\ln(\varepsilon^{-1})/M_{0}.

The lower bound on the right-hand side is larger than our time horizon
T
if
\varepsilon\in(0,e^{-M_{0}T/\delta_{*}^{\prime}})
. Thus for
\varepsilon
in that range, we have
\tau_{0}^{\varepsilon}>T
, so that

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\Delta^{\varepsilon}_{0}(t,z)|\leq\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M_{0}T},

for all
t\in[\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},T]
. By the triangle inequality, if
m_{1}^{\varepsilon}\coloneqq m_{0}+\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M_{0}T}
, this implies

(6.26)

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})\leq\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)|+\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\Delta^{\varepsilon}_{0}(t,z)|\leq m_{1}^{\varepsilon},

for all
t\in[\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},T]
, where
m_{1}^{\varepsilon}=m_{0}+\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M_{0}T}
. Next, we consider

\Delta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(t,z)=\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k-1}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k-1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}),\quad k\geq 1.

For
k\geq 1
, define

F^{\varepsilon}_{k}(z)\coloneqq\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{\underline{t}^{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime})\Delta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t_{k}^{\varepsilon},z^{\prime}),

and
\Omega_{2,k}\coloneqq\{\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|F^{\varepsilon}_{k}(z)|<\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}-\zeta}\}
.
The goal is to show that for each
k\in\{1,\cdots,\lfloor T/\varepsilon^{\beta}\rfloor\}
, we have on
\Omega_{2,k}\cap\Omega_{1}
the bounds

(6.27)

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\Delta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)|<\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M^{\varepsilon}_{k}T},

and

(6.28)

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})\leq m_{k+1}^{\varepsilon},

for all
t\in[t_{k}^{\varepsilon}+\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},T]
, where
m_{k+1}^{\varepsilon}=m_{k}^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M_{k}^{\varepsilon}T}
, with
M_{k}^{\varepsilon}=C(m_{k}^{\varepsilon}+1)^{2N}
, for some constant
C=C(T)
. We use induction on
k
, with both the base case
k=1
and the induction step following the approach used to control
\Delta_{0}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)
above. The main difference is that we replace the time-
0
control on the
v
-functions provided by our Assumption
A2
.a with an application of Lemma
37
. Let
t\coloneqq t_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\underline{t}^{\varepsilon}
. As before, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}(z,z^{\prime})\Delta_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t_{1},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{t}ds\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{t-s}(z,z^{\prime})[(\mu-\rho_{s-t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\Delta_{1}^{\varepsilon}(s,z^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{1}^{\varepsilon}(s,z^{\prime}))^{2}].

Using (
6.26
),
t-t_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\underline{t}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{1}
, the integral is bounded by
M_{1}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{(1-\beta/2)/2-2\xi}
, where
M^{\varepsilon}_{1}=C(m_{1}^{\varepsilon}+1)^{2}
for some
C=C(T)>0
. Define

F_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)\coloneqq\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G_{\underline{t}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})\Delta_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t_{1}^{\varepsilon},z^{\prime}).

By similar calculations as above, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}[(F_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z))^{2N}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{2N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x}=N\\
n_{\hat{x}}=r\end{subarray}}\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq\hat{x}}d^{\varepsilon}(\hat{x},x_{1};\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot\;;X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\left(\prod_{z^{\prime}\in x\backslash\hat{x}}G_{t-t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})\right)\left(\prod_{z^{\prime}\in\hat{x}}G_{t-t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z,z^{\prime})\right)v_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(x\backslash x_{1},\rho_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot\;;X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})|\nu^{\varepsilon}).

By Lemma
37
, there exists
c=c(T,N)>0
such that

v_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(x\backslash x_{1},\rho_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot\;;X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})|\nu^{\varepsilon})\leq c\varepsilon^{(2N-n_{x_{1}})\delta_{0}}<c\varepsilon^{(2N-r)\delta_{*}},

uniformly in
x
and
x_{1}\preccurlyeq\hat{x}
. By (
6.23
) and (
6.26
), we have

d(x,x_{1};\rho_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot;X_{t_{0}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\leq C(m_{1}^{\varepsilon}+1)^{2},

for some
C=C(T)>0
. We control the transition probabilities as we did for
\Delta_{0}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)
above.
Hence, the event
\Omega_{2,1}=\{\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|F_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)|<\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}-\zeta}\}
satisfies

\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2,1}^{c})\leq(m_{1}^{\varepsilon}+1)^{2N}C\varepsilon^{2N\zeta}\leq(m_{1}^{\varepsilon}+1)^{2N}C\varepsilon^{q}

for some for some
C=C(T,N)>0
. Moreover, on
\Omega_{2,1}\cap\Omega_{1}
, we get

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}|\Delta_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,z)|<\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M^{\varepsilon}_{1}T},

for all
t\in[t_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},T]
. As in (
6.26
), we have by the triangle inequality that

\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{1}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})\leq m_{2}^{\varepsilon},

for all
t\in[\underline{t}^{\varepsilon},T]
, where
m_{2}^{\varepsilon}=m_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}e^{M_{1}^{\varepsilon}T}
. The method used to show the
k=1
case applies to prove the induction step. Thus, in particular, (
6.27
) and (
6.28
) hold for all
k\in\{1,\cdots,\lfloor T/\varepsilon^{\beta}\rfloor\}
. To bound the right-hand side of (
6.27
) by
c\varepsilon^{\delta_{*}^{\prime}}
for some
c=c(T,N)>0
independent of
k
or
\varepsilon
, we must check that
M_{k}^{\varepsilon}
remains bounded uniformly in
\varepsilon
as
k
becomes large. It is easily seen that the arguments at the end of the proof of Proposition 4.2 of
[
Boldrighini et al., 1992
]
apply since
\delta_{*}>\beta
, hence
\delta_{\star}^{\prime}>\beta
. Define

\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{1}\cap\bigcap_{k\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor T/\varepsilon^{\beta}\rfloor\}}\Omega_{2,k}.

Then,
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2})>1-c\varepsilon^{q}
for some
c=c(T,N)
, and if
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{\star}^{\prime}-\beta)
and
\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}
, we obtain the bound

|\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X^{\varepsilon}_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}})-\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{k}|\Delta^{\varepsilon}_{j}(t,z)|\leq c_{1}Te^{c_{2}T}\varepsilon^{\delta_{\star}^{\prime}-\beta}<c_{3}\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}},

for some
c_{1}=c_{1}(T,N),c_{2}(T,N),c_{3}(T,N)>0
, for all
k\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor T/\varepsilon^{\beta}\rfloor\}
and
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
. Since
0<\delta_{\star}^{\prime}<\delta_{\star}-\zeta
, and
\zeta>0
is arbitrary, this proves the lemma.
∎

Remark 43
.

Note that in the inequality (
6.24
), we have used the crude lower bound
1+\kappa\geq 1
. The reason is that in choosing
\delta_{*}
in the proof of Lemma
42
, we are forced to consider the worst case of (
6.1
) and (
6.2
). This is however not an issue for the proof of Theorem
21
below. Indeed, following the method of
[
Boldrighini et al., 1992
]
in the proof of their Lemma 1, we will first take
n
large enough that the control claimed in Theorem
21
holds, and then we will use the hierarchy of equations for the
v
-functions and Assumption
A2
.a to prove the result for lower order
v
-functions - see (
6.32
) below. We note here that one crucial difference between the proofs of Theorem
21
and Lemma 1 of
[
Boldrighini et al., 1992
]
which allows us to obtain a stronger rate of convergence in terms of the moments of the offpsring distribution and the competition exponent
\kappa
is that the coefficients of the hierarchy of equations for low order
v
-functions are small in
\varepsilon^{\kappa}
. See Proposition
22
.

Proof of Theorem
21
.

This proof follows that of Lemma 1 in
[
Boldrighini et al., 1992
]
. Let
k\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor T/\varepsilon^{\beta}\rfloor\}
, and
t\in[t_{k+1}^{\varepsilon},t_{k+2}^{\varepsilon}]
. Consider a test configuration
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
n_{x}=n
for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. By Definition
12
, we have

\displaystyle V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\coloneqq\sum_{x_{0}\preccurlyeq x}Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{0},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})(-1)^{n_{x}-n_{x_{0}}}\prod_{z\in x\backslash x_{0}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z).

Since

\displaystyle\prod_{z\in x\backslash x_{0}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{z\in x\backslash x_{0}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})+\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x\backslash x_{0}}\prod_{z\in x\backslash(x_{0}\cup x_{1})}\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})\prod_{z\in x_{1}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))<c,

we can write

\displaystyle V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{0}\preccurlyeq x}Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{0},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})(-1)^{x\backslash x_{0}}\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x\backslash x_{0}}\prod_{z\in x\backslash(x_{0}\cup x_{1})}\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\prod_{z\in x_{1}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})).

We interchange the order of summation to obtain

\displaystyle V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}(-1)^{n_{x_{1}}}\left(\sum_{x_{0}\preccurlyeq x\backslash x_{1}}Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{0},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})(-1)^{n_{x}-n_{x_{0}}-n_{x_{1}}}\prod_{z\in x\backslash(x_{0}\cup x_{1})}\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\prod_{z\in x_{1}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}(-1)^{n_{x_{1}}}V^{\varepsilon}(x\backslash x_{1},X_{t}^{\varepsilon},\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\prod_{z\in x_{1}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})).

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}
and choose
r
,
\beta
,
\xi
, and
\delta
as in Lemma
42
. Then, using the Markov property, Lemma
37
, and Lemma
42
, we get almost surely,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(Z,X_{t};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\Big|X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}(-1)^{n_{x_{1}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x\backslash x_{1},X_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon},\rho_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(\cdot;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\right]\prod_{z\in x_{1}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}|v_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}(x\backslash x_{1}|X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon})|\left|\prod_{z\in x_{1}}(\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t-t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}(z;X_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}))\right|

(6.29)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}\varepsilon^{(n-n_{x_{1}})\delta_{0}}\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}n_{x_{1}}}=C\left(\varepsilon^{\delta_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}}\right)^{n}<c\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}n},

for some
C=C(T,n)>0
, and where
c=c(T,n)\coloneqq 2^{n}C
. By the tower law of expectations and the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})]|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\Big|X^{\varepsilon}_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}}\right]\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\Big|X^{\varepsilon}_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}^{c}}\right]\right|.

We control the first term using (
6.29
):

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\Big|X^{\varepsilon}_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}}\right]\right|<c\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}n}.

For the second term, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz and Jensen inequalities, yielding the bound

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\Big|X^{\varepsilon}_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}^{c}}\right]\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}^{c})^{1/2}.

By another application of Jensen’s inequality, we compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{2}]

\displaystyle=2^{2n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2^{-n}\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}(-1)^{n_{x}-n_{x_{1}}}Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})\prod_{z\in x\backslash x_{1}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n}\sum_{x_{1}\preccurlyeq x}\mathbb{E}\left[Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\right]\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2(n-n_{x_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq 4^{n}\left(\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}\leq n\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\right]\right)\times\left(\max_{i\in\{0,\cdots,n\}}\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2(n-i)}\right).

Using Corollary
28
, we have
\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}\leq n\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[Q^{\varepsilon}(x_{1},X_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\right]\leq C_{1}
uniformly in
\varepsilon
, for some constant
C_{1}=C_{1}(T,n)>0
. By part three of Lemma
15
, there exists
C_{2}=C_{2}(T,n)>0
such that
\max_{i\in\{0,\cdots,n\}}\max_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z)^{2(n-i)}\leq C_{2}
. Combined with Lemma
42
, we obtain the existence of a constant
C=C(T,n,p)>0
such that

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\varepsilon}(x,X_{t}^{\varepsilon};\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon})\Big|X^{\varepsilon}_{t_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{X}^{\varepsilon}\in\Omega_{2}^{c}}\right]\right|\leq C\varepsilon^{q/2}.

By choosing
q>2\delta_{1}n
in Lemma
42
and gathering the above estimates, we obtain, for some
c=c(T,n)>0
, the bound

(6.30)

|v_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|<c\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}n},

for all configurations
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
n_{x}=n
, and
t\in[t_{1}^{\varepsilon},T]
. If
t\in[0,t_{1}^{\varepsilon}]
, by (
6.2
) Lemma
37
, there exists
c=c(T,n)>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,t_{1}^{\varepsilon}]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x}=n\end{subarray}}|v_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|<c\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}n(1+\kappa)}.

Define
n_{0}\coloneqq(1+\kappa)\lceil 2/\delta_{1}\rceil
. From Lemma
42
, we have
\delta_{1}<1/2
, so that
n_{0}\geq 4(1+\lceil\kappa\rceil)=8
. If
n\geq n_{0}
, we have

(6.31)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x}=n\end{subarray}}v^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}),

for some
c=c(T,n)>0
, as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. Suppose now that
n=n_{0}-1
. By (
2.2
) in Proposition
22
, Assumption
A2
.a, and the triangle inequality, we obtain

(6.32)

\displaystyle|v_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|

\displaystyle\leq c\varepsilon^{\lfloor(n+1)/2\rfloor(1+\kappa)}

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{t}ds\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x_{1}}=n_{x}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-s}(x,x_{1})\sum_{z\in\operatorname{supp}(x_{1})}\sum_{h=-n_{x_{1}}(z)}^{1}c_{h}^{\varepsilon}(n_{x_{1}}(z),\rho_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z))v_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z,h)}|\nu^{\varepsilon})\right|,

for all
x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}
with
n_{x}=n
, and
t\in[0,T]
, for some
c=c(T,n)>0
. By (
2.3
) in Proposition
22
, part two of Lemma
15
, the
c_{h}^{\varepsilon}
are bounded in absolute value, uniformly in all their parameters. If
h=1
in the inner-most summation, then
x_{1}^{(z,h)}
is comprised of
n_{0}
particles, and (
6.31
) shows that the integral is
o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})
. If
h=-1
, then by Proposition
22
, there are at least two particles locally, and using (
C.2
), we obtain a contribution of
O(\sqrt{t}\varepsilon^{1+\delta_{1}(n_{0}-1)})
. Since

1+\delta_{1}(n_{0}-1)\geq 2(1+\kappa)-\delta_{1}\geq 3/2+2\kappa,

we have
O(\sqrt{t}\varepsilon^{1+\delta_{1}(n_{0}-1)})=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})
as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. For
h\in\{-2,-3,-4\}
, we get using the same method
O(\sqrt{t}\varepsilon^{1+(n_{0}+h)\delta_{1}})=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})
as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. In fact, for any
h\leq-3
where there are at least three particles locally, using (
C.2
) and the fact that
\kappa\in[0,3/8]
, we obtain that the integral is

O\left(\varepsilon^{1+2\kappa}+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\varepsilon^{1+2\kappa}}^{t}s^{-1}ds\right)=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}),

as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. By Grönwall’s inequality, we get

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x}=n_{0}-1\end{subarray}}|v^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}).

By taking
\beta\in(0,6/15)
sufficiently small, we can always get
\delta_{1}
to approximate
3/8
from below arbitrarily well. In particular, we require
\delta_{1}>\kappa
. Then if
\kappa\in[0,3/8]
, we may repeat the arguments above starting from
n=n_{0}-2
and down to
n=3
, and obtain for all
n\geq 3
that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}\\
n_{x}=n\end{subarray}}|v^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|=o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}).

as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
with
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Next, consider
n=2
. Let
p\in[1,2]
be such that the offspring distribution
\mathrm{P}
has a finite moment of order
p
. If
h=1
in (
6.32
), we are back in the case
n=3
, and we obtain a contribution of
o(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})
. Let now
h=-1
. By Proposition
22
, if
m=1
and
h\leq-1
, we have
c_{h}^{\varepsilon}(m,u)=0
. Therefore, we may assume that
x_{1}=2\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z^{\prime}\}}
for some
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
. In that case, if
x
is comprised of two particles at some locations
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
, then by (
C.2
), we get

(6.33)

\mathbb{G}^{\varepsilon}_{t-s}(x,x_{1})\leq CG^{\varepsilon}_{t-s}(z_{1},z^{\prime})\varepsilon/\sqrt{t-s},

for some
C=C(T)>0
, and where
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. By (
6.30
) with
n=1
, we get
v_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}^{(z^{\prime},-1)}|\nu^{\varepsilon})=O(\varepsilon^{\delta_{1}})=o(\varepsilon^{\kappa})
. Now by (
2.5
) of Proposition
22
, and part two of Lemma
15
, we obtain
c^{\varepsilon}_{-1}(2,\rho_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z))=O(\varepsilon^{\kappa})+o(\varepsilon^{(p-1)\kappa})
uniformly in
s
and
z
. Thus using that
\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}}G^{\varepsilon}_{t-s}(z_{1},z^{\prime})=1
and
\int_{0}^{t}ds/{\sqrt{t-s}}=2\sqrt{t}
, the integral of the
h=-1
summand in (
6.32
) contributes
o(\varepsilon^{1+(p-1)\kappa})
as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. Finally, we consider the case
h=-2
, where
x_{1}=2\varepsilon^{\kappa}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z^{\prime}\}}
for some
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\varepsilon}
and both particles are removed. We begin by recalling that the
v
-function on the test configuration
x\equiv 0
is equal to 1, by Definitions (
13
) and (
12
). We again apply (
6.33
) which gives us a factor of
\varepsilon/\sqrt{t-s}
. Additionally, by (
2.4
) of Proposition
22
, and part two of Lemma
15
, the coefficient
c_{-2}^{\varepsilon}(2,\rho_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z))
is
O(\varepsilon^{\kappa})+o(\varepsilon^{(p-1)\kappa})
uniformly in
s
and
z
. Thus, overall the contribution of the
h=-2
case is
O(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})+o(\varepsilon^{1+(p-1)\kappa})
with a constant depending on
T
,
n=2
, and
p
. By Grönwall’s inequality, we obtain

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}:n_{x}=2}|v_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|\leq C\left[O(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa})+o(\varepsilon^{1+(p-1)\kappa})\right],

for some
C=C(T,n,p)>0
, as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. It follows that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}:n_{x}=2}|v_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|\leq\begin{cases}o(\varepsilon^{1+(p-1)\kappa})&p\in(1,2),\\
C\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}&p=2.\end{cases}

If now
n=1
, we consider (
6.32
) with
n_{x}=1
. The summand
h=1
in (
6.32
) contributes the right-hand side of the last displayed expression, and by Proposition
22
,
c_{h}^{\varepsilon}(1,u)=0
for all
h\leq-1
. Hence by Grönwall’s inequality, we obtain

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{x\in\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}:n_{x}=1}|v_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x|\nu^{\varepsilon})|\leq\begin{cases}o(\varepsilon^{1+(p-1)\kappa})&p\in(1,2),\\
C\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}&p=2,\end{cases}

for some
C=C(T,n,p)>0
, and for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. This proves Theorem
21
.
∎

7.
